# A high-arity counterexample to Pixton completeness in Chow

Coordinate `EN-103-000-0000-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M3-C-TO-H-0129` | `PM-053.1` | status ACTIVE | original slug `A-high-arity-counterexample-to-Pixton-completeness-in-Chow-September-24-2026` | date 2026-09-24 | derived from the openai/math preprint A-high-arity-counterexample-to-Pixton-completeness-in-Chow-September-24-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We disprove the Chow and rational-cohomological forms of Pixton's completeness conjecture for his original relation system. We construct a formal tautological class outside the original Pixton relation span whose image is zero in the Chow ring and in rational cohomology. The example has genus $`10^{60}`$ and $`3\\binom{10^{60}}3`$ markings.",
```

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We disprove the Chow and rational-cohomological forms of Pixton's completeness conjecture for his original relation system. We construct a formal tautological class outside the original Pixton relation span whose image is zero in the Chow ring and in rational cohomology. The example has genus $`10^{60}`$ and $`3\binom{10^{60}}3`$ markings.

- **claim-main-theorem** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Set $$g=10^{60},\qquad D_*=\binom g3,\qquad n=3D_*.$$ The explicitly defined class $Y\in S_{g,n}^{2D_*}$ of Definition <a href="#def:candidate" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:candidate">5</a> satisfies $$q_{g,n}(Y)=0,\qquad Y\notin\mathcal P_{g,n}.$$ Consequently Pixton’s original relation span does not exhaust the tautological Chow relations: the equality $\ker q_{g,n}=\mathcal P_{g,n}$ fails for this stable pair.
  - Explanation: For the fixed huge stable pair, the explicit formal class Y realizes to zero in Chow yet lies outside Pixton's original span, so Chow completeness fails.
- **claim-cohomological-incompleteness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-cohomological-incompleteness)): For the stable pair and class $Y$ of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, $$q^H_{g,n}(Y)=0 \quad\text{in }H^{4D_*}\!\left(\overline{\mathcal M}_{g,n}(\mathbb C);\mathbb Q\right), \qquad Y\notin\mathcal P_{g,n}.$$ In particular, $\mathcal P_{g,n}\subsetneq\ker q^H_{g,n}$.
  - Explanation: The cycle class map sends the Chow vanishing to cohomological vanishing while nonmembership stays, so the cohomological form fails too.
- **claim-known-relations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-known-relations)): The span in Definition <a href="#def:pixton-span" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:pixton-span">3</a> is an ideal and is stable under tautological gluing and forgetting pushforwards and pullbacks. For gluing pullback to a product, the target relation space is the sum of the spaces with a relation in one factor. Moreover $$\mathcal P_{g,n}\subseteq\ker q_{g,n}.$$
  - Explanation: The original span is an ideal stable under gluing and forgetting operations and lies in the Chow kernel.
- **claim-detector-existence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-detector)): There are a supercommutative $\mathbb Q$-algebra $R$, a finite free supermodule $V$ over $R$, and $\mathbb Q$-linear maps $$t_m:S_{g,m}\longrightarrow V^{\otimes_R m} \qquad(m\geq 0)$$ such that $t_m(\mathcal P_{g,m})=0$ for every $m$, whereas $t_n(Y)\neq0$. In particular $Y\notin\mathcal P_{g,n}$.
  - Explanation: Linear tests into tensor powers of a supermodule over an exterior algebra kill the whole Pixton span but not Y, proving formal nonmembership.
- **claim-detector-algebra** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-detector-algebra)): These rules give a supercommutative associative Frobenius algebra. The product of three standard odd vectors belongs to $Re$, and the product of four such vectors is zero. Its handle element is $$\mu\Delta(1)=(2-2g)e.$$
  - Explanation: The isotropic cubic rules give a supercommutative associative Frobenius algebra with a computed handle element.
- **claim-detector-cutoff** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-detector-cutoff)): The prescription is well defined modulo genuine relations on rational parameter factors and induces a map on $S_{g,m}$. For a homogeneous expression of degree $r$, its image is a sum of tensors indexed by partitions of the labels into nonempty blocks, of the form $$\bigotimes_{L}\Delta_L(\lambda^{d_L}), \qquad r=\sum_L\bigl(\|L\|-1+d_L\bigr). \label{eq:detector-blocks}$$ In particular $t_m$ is zero in degrees $r>m$.
  - Explanation: The rational-tails compression is well defined, descends to the truncated algebra, and vanishes above the label count.
- **claim-detector-operations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-detector-operations)): The maps $t_m$ are equivariant for relabeling. For every strata expression $b$, they satisfy $$\begin{aligned} t(\bar\psi_i b)&=\lambda_i t(b), &t(\bar\kappa_j b)&=0, \label{eq:detector-bars}\\ t(\bar D_{ij}b)&=\Delta_{ij}(1)t(b). \label{eq:detector-pair} \end{aligned}$$ More generally, gluing along $D_T$ integrates the entire genus-zero input and duplicates the root-side attaching slot into the labels of $T$.
  - Explanation: The tests are equivariant; bar cotangent classes act by the handle scalar, bar kappa classes by zero, tail pairs by the coproduct.
- **claim-detector-forgetting** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-detector-forgetting)): For every strata expression $b$, the maps $t_m$ satisfy $$t(\pi_y^*b)=t(b)\otimes1, \qquad t(\pi_{y*}b)=\int_y t(b). \label{eq:detector-forget}$$ Here $\pi_y$ forgets the label $y$; the identities hold in any order of the remaining labels.
  - Explanation: Forgetting pullback tensors with the unit and forgetting pushforward integrates the slot.
- **claim-detector-unit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-detector-unit)): If all preceding leg indices $a_j$ are zero or one, then, in the untruncated rational-tails computation, $$\mathcal R(s;a,0) -\sum_\ell s_\ell\mathcal R(s;a,\ell) =\pi_y^*\mathcal R(s;a). \label{eq:detector-unit}$$
  - Explanation: Adding a zero-index leg to packaged Pixton recipes gives a pullback identity through a combination over allowed parts.
- **claim-detector-annihilation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-detector-annihilation)): For every $m\geq0$, $t_m(\mathcal P_{g,m})=0$.
  - Explanation: Induction on arity, degree and partition length shows every test kills the original span.
- **claim-tensor-test** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-tensor-test)): In the setup above, either in relative rational Chow or in the indicated quotient by base support, there is a supercommutative rational algebra $\mathfrak B$ with the following properties.* 1. *The curve correspondence algebra has a faithful tensor model on $$\mathfrak B\otimes \bigl(\mathbb Q1\oplus W_b\oplus\mathbb Qe\bigr),$$ where $1,e$ are even and $W_b$ is an odd symplectic space of dimension $2b$. The classes $1_C,e'_C$ correspond to $1,e$, and $\eta$ projects onto $W_b$. Integration gives the hyperbolic pairing $\langle1,e\rangle=1$, $\langle1,1\rangle=\langle e,e\rangle=0$ on the two even summands and the symplectic pairing on $W_b$. All actual Chow correspondences between curve powers, not only pairing diagrams, are included faithfully in this model.* 2. *The projected small diagonal is an alternating three-form $$\varphi\colon W_b^3\longrightarrow\mathfrak B_{\mathrm{odd}}.$$ Cup product, integration, tensor products, permutations, and contractions are computed in the model with the Koszul signs.* 3. *Let $U$ be a finite-dimensional ordinary rational color space. A pure colored tensor in $$\label{eq:colored-chow-space} \left( \eta^{\otimes k}A^*(C^k) \otimes U^{\otimes k}\otimes\mathrm{sgn}_k \right)_{\mathfrak S_k}$$ is zero if and only if all its evaluations using the same ordinary linear map $z\colon U\to W_b$ in every slot are zero in $\mathfrak B$. Finitely many rational maps $z$ suffice for each fixed $U,k$. The assertion holds in the base-support quotient as well.* *In particular, a product of projected cubic entries is tested by products of $\varphi(z(i),z(j),z(k))$, with the fixed Koszul sign coming from the ordering of the factors. A vanishing established by these tests is a vanishing in Chow, with the stated base-support bound.
  - Explanation: Curve correspondences have a faithful tensor model; the projected small diagonal is an odd alternating three-form; colored pure tensors are detected by same-map evaluations.
- **claim-relative-pfaffian** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-relative-pfaffian)): On $C^{2b+2}$, one has $$\label{eq:relative-pfaffian} \sum_{\mathcal M} \prod_{\{i,j\}\in\mathcal M}\eta_{ij}=0,$$ where $\mathcal M$ ranges over the perfect matchings of $\{1,\ldots,2b+2\}$.
  - Explanation: The sum over perfect matchings of products of the projector vanishes on the curve power, via Fourier transform on the Jacobian.
- **claim-coefficient-algebra** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-coefficient-algebra)): Let $F$ be the functor in <a href="#eq:symplectic-chow-functor" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:symplectic-chow-functor">[eq:symplectic-chow-functor]</a>, and put $H(X)=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathcal C}(1,F(X))$. There is a supercommutative $\mathop{\mathrm{Sp}}_{2b}$-algebra $\mathfrak B$ such that, naturally in $X$, $$\label{eq:coefficient-invariants} H(X)\simeq(\mathfrak B\otimes X)^{\mathop{\mathrm{Sp}}_{2b}}.$$ Under these identifications, tensor products of target morphisms become multiplication of coefficients. Arbitrary morphisms $F(X)\to F(Y)$ are represented faithfully by equivariant $\mathfrak B$-linear maps $\mathfrak B\otimes X\to\mathfrak B\otimes Y$, and their composition is the usual tensor contraction.
  - Explanation: A supercommutative symplectic coefficient algebra represents all correspondence morphisms faithfully.
- **claim-point-centering** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-point-centered-algebra)): Work modulo positive-codimension base support, and suppose $e_C$ is the class of a section. Then $e'_C=e_C$ in the quotient. In the model of Proposition <a href="#prop:tensor-test" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:tensor-test">13</a>, the functional $$\epsilon(x)=\int_C e_Cx$$ is multiplicative and satisfies $$\epsilon(1)=1,\qquad \epsilon(e)=0,\qquad \epsilon(W_b)=0.$$ Consequently the component of a product $w_1w_2$, with $w_1,w_2\in W_b$, in the two even summands is $\langle w_1,w_2\rangle e$.
  - Explanation: Centered at a section and modulo base support, evaluation at the section is multiplicative and kills the odd summand.
- **claim-color-polarization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-color-polarization)): In the pure subspace $\eta^{\otimes k}\mathcal A_k(U)$, evaluations using the same scalar map $z\colon U\to W_b$ in every slot are jointly injective. For fixed $k,U$, a finite collection of rational maps is jointly injective. These statements hold after quotienting by base support of any prescribed positive codimension.
  - Explanation: Pure colored classes are determined by evaluations with one scalar color map in every slot; finitely many maps suffice.
- **claim-surface-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-surface)): Suppose $b\geq5$ and $\dim S<\chi-1$. Let $(w_\alpha)_{\alpha\in I}$ be any finite collection of vectors in the odd symplectic standard space $W_b$ whose span is isotropic. For any matrix of scalars $\gamma=(\gamma_{\alpha\beta})_{\alpha,\beta\in I}$, put $$\label{eq:surface-tensor} T_\gamma= \sum_{\substack{\alpha_1,\alpha_2,\alpha_3\in I\\ \beta_1,\beta_2,\beta_3\in I}} \left(\prod_{j=1}^3\gamma_{\alpha_j\beta_j}\right) \varphi(w_{\alpha_1},w_{\alpha_2},w_{\alpha_3}) \varphi(w_{\beta_1},w_{\beta_2},w_{\beta_3}).$$ Then $$\label{eq:surface-bound} T_\gamma^{\,t_0}=0\qquad\text{in }\mathfrak B.$$ The assertion remains valid after any characteristic-zero scalar extension of the tensor formalism.
  - Explanation: A power of the quadratic expression in the projected cubic on isotropic vectors vanishes modulo base support.
- **claim-quot-arrow** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-quot-arrow)): The derived stack of arrows with target $V$ has an open derived substack whose classical truncation is the ordinary relative Quot scheme with the prescribed Hilbert polynomial, before fixing determinant. On this truncation its relative tangent complex is $$\operatorname{Cone}\bigl( \mathbf R\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(I',I')\longrightarrow \mathbf R\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(I',V)\bigr) \simeq \mathbf R\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(I',Q).$$ This is a perfect complex of Tor-amplitude $[0,1]$, compatible with base change. The differential of forgetting the arrow and keeping its source is the boundary map $$\mathbf R\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(I',Q)\longrightarrow\mathbf R\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(I',I')[1].$$
  - Explanation: An open derived arrow substack truncates to the relative Quot scheme with a perfect two-term tangent complex.
- **claim-det-surjective** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-det-surjective)): Let $0\to F\to V_0\to Q_0\to0$ be an exact sequence on a smooth projective surface, where $V_0$ is a vector bundle of rank $r$ and $Q_0$ is torsion. If $K$ is ample and $Kc_1(Q_0)>rK^2$, the map $$\mathop{\mathrm{Ext}}^1(F,Q_0)\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Ext}}^2(F,F) \xrightarrow{\mathrm{tr}}H^2(\mathcal O_X)$$ is surjective.
  - Explanation: Under an ampleness inequality, the determinant obstruction map onto top cohomology of the structure sheaf is surjective.
- **claim-point-excess** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-point-excess)): The unrestricted low rank-one Quot scheme is classically $$\operatorname{Div}_{P}(X/S)\times_S X^{[r_{\mathrm{low}}]}_{/S},$$ where the divisor scheme permits its line to vary in the prescribed Picard data. Its virtual class is the divisor virtual class times the smooth relative point-Hilbert class, capped with $$\label{eq:point-obstruction} e(F_{\mathrm{low}}),\qquad F_i=\left(\pi_*\mathcal O_{Z_i}(K-\mathcal C_i)\right)^\vee.$$
  - Explanation: The low rank-one Quot scheme is a divisor scheme times a smooth point Hilbert scheme, with virtual class capped by an excess Euler class.
- **claim-marked-universality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-marked-universality)): Fix finitely many marked output surface factors. Push forward, over relative point Hilbert schemes and any additional surface factors, a finite polynomial in Chern characters of universal point ideals, their derived duals, line classes, relative tangent Chern classes, and equality diagonals. The resulting Chow class is a universal finite sum of decorated equality diagonals among the outputs, multiplied by relative characteristic pushes from closed surface components. One fixed polynomial procedure chooses the expression, independently of the surface family. No marked output is integrated in the procedure.
  - Explanation: Marked pushforwards from point Hilbert schemes follow one universal procedure in decorated equality diagonals.
- **claim-localization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-localization)): Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:surface" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:surface">18</a>, the scalar $a_{N,t_0}$ satisfies $$a_{N,t_0}\,T_\gamma^{\,t_0}=0 \qquad\text{in }\mathfrak B_{S,b-1}.$$
  - Explanation: Virtual localization forces the scalar times the cubic contraction power to vanish modulo base support.
- **claim-scalar-nonzero** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-scalar)): The bare-triple scalar $a_{N,t_0}$ in Proposition <a href="#prop:localization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:localization">23</a> is nonzero. Consequently Theorem <a href="#thm:surface" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:surface">18</a> holds.
  - Explanation: A residue computation shows the bare-triple scalar is nonzero, completing the surface bound.
- **claim-moment-additivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-moment-additivity)): There is a series $c(q,x_e,x_m)$ such that $$\label{eq:theta-exponential} \mathcal E(\Theta) = \mathcal E(\Theta\|_{p=0}) \exp\bigl(c(q,x_e,x_m)p^3/3!\bigr).$$ Moreover, let $c_H(q)$ be the coefficient of $M_3(H)$, with the factor $p^3/3!$ removed, in the one-moment part of $\log\Theta(q,p,0,0)$. Then $$\label{eq:shift-derivative} [x_e]c(q,x_e,0)=-c_H(q).$$
  - Explanation: The marked series exponentiates a single triple coefficient, and the Euler-shift derivative identifies it with the divisor coefficient.
- **claim-exterior-ideal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-exterior-ideal)): Let $F\subset H$ be vector spaces of dimensions $2r$ and $D$, and let $\Omega\in\bigwedge^2F$ be nondegenerate. If $0\le t\le r$, then the ideal generated by $\Omega^t$ in $\bigwedge H$ contains $\bigwedge^qH$ for every $q\ge D-r+t$.
  - Explanation: A power of a nondegenerate bivector generates all sufficiently high exterior degrees.
- **claim-large-vertex-test** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-large-vertex-test)): Let a smooth curve family of genus $b\ge5$, with a section, be over a smooth irreducible quasi-projective base $S$ satisfying $\dim S<(b-1)^2-1$. Put $$t_b=25(b-1)^2-1.$$ Let $E$ be an $m$-dimensional color space, and let $e$ be an even integer with $e\ge m/2-2$ and $e\le m/2$. Write $D_E=\binom m3$ and $d_e=\binom e3$. If $t_b\le d_e/2$, every product of $$q\ge D_E-d_e/2+t_b$$ pure cubic entries whose colors lie in $E$ vanishes modulo support in base codimension at least $b-1$.
  - Explanation: Long products of pure cubic entries with arbitrary colors vanish modulo base support.
- **claim-weighted-pullback** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-weighted-pullback)): Let $g\geq2$, let the heavy set be empty, and pull the three-light operations to any larger number of light labels by forgetting. Under weight reduction from ordinary stable pointed curves, $[i=j]$ pulls back to $\bar D_{ij}$ and $\beta_{g,\varnothing}$ pulls back to the normalized two-edge bridge sum used to define $\beta_J^{\mathrm{st}}$. Consequently the coefficient of the prescribed color word in the signed product of all $F^0_{g,\varnothing}$ pulls back to $q(Y)$, up to a nonzero rational scalar.
  - Explanation: Weight reduction pulls coincidence and bridge classes back to tail divisors and bridge sums, so the weighted product pulls back to the realization of Y up to a nonzero scalar.
- **claim-three-position-blowup** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-three-position-blowup)): The map <a href="#eq:coarse-positions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:coarse-positions">[eq:coarse-positions]</a> is the iterated blowup obtained, at each flag $p$, by first blowing up the locus where all three assigned positions equal $p$, when present, and then the strict transforms of the loci where two assigned positions equal $p$. The latter centers are disjoint after the first blowup.
  - Explanation: The coarse position map is an iterated blowup along triple then strict pair coincidence loci.
- **claim-triple-cancellation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-triple-exceptional-cancellation)): For the pullback of a raw cubic class $F^0$ from an ancestor chart, the coefficient $a_p$ in <a href="#eq:blowup-components" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:blowup-components">[eq:blowup-components]</a> is zero at every triple flag locus.
  - Explanation: The scalar of the squared triple exceptional divisor vanishes since coincidence cancels against bridge or heavy-tail corrections.
- **claim-controlled-lifts** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-controlled-lifts)): For every stable heavy type $(h,B)$ one can choose a symmetric class $G_{h,B}\in A^2(X_{h,B}(3))$ with the following properties.* 1. *Over the smooth heavy base, $G_{h,B}$ is exactly the pullback of $\Delta_C^{\mathrm{pure}}$ by the coarse-position map.* 2. *Pull a raw cubic $F^0$, or one of these chosen pure lifts $G$, from any ancestor chart to a compact descendant cut chart. Its operation is a sum of the chosen pure lifts at the relevant descendant vertices, with all three labels assigned to that vertex, together with pair-times-singleton operations and a residual cubic operation.* 3. *The singleton operations in <span class="upright">(ii)</span>, and all their further pullbacks through cuts and light forgetting, are controlled as follows. Over fixed smooth core data their restrictions lie componentwise in the span of the unit, the canonical class, and the flag points. On any smooth test family the same statement holds modulo classes supported over positive base codimension.* 4. *On the current smooth-core open the residual operation in <span class="upright">(ii)</span> is a finite sum of classes containing a pulled base divisor factor. The base divisor may come from the current heavy base or from a compact ancestor base. Consequently its pullback to every smooth test family is zero operationally after removing a positive-codimension subset of that test base.* *All expansions preserve the color multiset. They may always use one and the same chosen $G$ and centering at a given current vertex, regardless of which ancestor supplied the operation.
  - Explanation: Symmetric pure lifts exist on weighted spaces, match the projected diagonal on the smooth core, and decompose under cuts into pure lifts, controlled pair-singleton terms and base-divisor residuals.
- **claim-support-advancement** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-support-advancement)): Let a cycle multiplier on $X^\Gamma(n)$ have base image contained in an irreducible closed subspace $Z\subset\mathcal B_\Gamma$. After passing to a refinement if necessary, suppose that its generic base image lies in $\mathcal S_\Gamma$. Let $L$ be a factor involving a specified subset of the active entries.* *Suppose that the pull of $L$ vanishes operationally over a dense open of a smooth test family dominating $Z\cap\mathcal S_\Gamma$. Then its action can be written as a finite sum of cycles with strictly smaller base-image dimension. Only the entries in $L$ are consumed.* *More precisely, take a smooth test base $S$, proper and generically finite over $Z\cap\mathcal S_\Gamma$. If the vanishing holds off base codimension at least $a$, the terms remaining over $\mathcal S_\Gamma$ have base-image dimension at most $\dim Z-a$. The other terms lie over $Z\setminus\mathcal S_\Gamma$ and require a strict graph refinement. If one first replaces $S$ by the total space of one of its curve families to obtain a section, the corresponding bound is $\dim Z-a+1$.
  - Explanation: Operational vanishing of a factor lets the cycle action split into cycles of strictly smaller base-image dimension.
- **claim-entry-tests** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-elementary-entry-tests)): Set $$R_\Gamma=2\|V(\Gamma)\|+2\|E(\Gamma)\|.$$ If $R_\Gamma+1$ noise entries have their singleton at the same color, their product advances support while consuming those entries. If a genus-$h$ vertex has more than $\binom{2h}{3}$ pure entries, the product of any $\binom{2h}{3}+1$ of them advances support.
  - Explanation: Enough noise entries with a common singleton color, or enough pure entries at a small-genus vertex, advance support.
- **claim-repeated-color** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-repeated-color)): Suppose that a vertex of genus $h$ has a flag and that $k>\binom{h+1}{2}$ pure entries contain one common color $i$. Their product is a finite linear combination of products $L*H$ with the following properties:* 1. *$L$ involves at most $h+2$ entries and vanishes modulo positive base support;* 2. *$H$ is still a product of individual pure entries at that vertex;* 3. *before removing the entries in $L$, each term has the same color multiset as the original product.* *Consequently each term admits a support advancement consuming at most $h+2$ entries.
  - Explanation: Many pure entries sharing a color at a flagged vertex factor into a short vanishing piece and a remainder, giving a cheap advancement.
- **claim-chow-vanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-chow-vanishing)): The class $Y\in S_{g,n}$ constructed above satisfies $$q_{g,n}(Y)=0 \quad\text{in }A^*(\overline{\mathcal M}_{g,n};\mathbb Q).$$
  - Explanation: A finite support induction exhausts the base support, so Y realizes to zero in rational Chow.

```json
        "exact_quote": "The span in Definition <a href=\"#def:pixton-span\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:pixton-span\">3</a> is an ideal and is stable under tautological gluing and forgetting pushforwards and pullbacks.         "exact_quote": "The prescription is well defined modulo genuine relations on rational parameter factors and induces a map on $S_{g,m}$.         "source_id": "PM-053.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-tensor-test",
        "exact_quote": "In the setup above, either in relative rational Chow or in the indicated quotient by base support, there is a supercommutative rational algebra $\\mathfrak B$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *The curve correspondence algebra has a faithful tensor model on $$\\mathfrak B\\otimes\n     \\bigl(\\mathbb Q1\\oplus W_b\\oplus\\mathbb Qe\\bigr),$$ where $1,e$ are even and $W_b$ is an odd symplectic space of dimension $2b$. The classes $1_C,e'_C$ correspond to $1,e$, and $\\eta$ projects onto $W_b$. Integration gives the hyperbolic pairing $\\langle1,e\\rangle=1$, $\\langle1,1\\rangle=\\langle e,e\\rangle=0$ on the two even summands and the symplectic pairing on $W_b$. All actual Chow correspondences between curve powers, not only pairing diagrams, are included faithfully in this model.*\n\n2.  *The projected small diagonal is an alternating three-form $$\\varphi\\colon W_b^3\\longrightarrow\\mathfrak B_{\\mathrm{odd}}.$$ Cup product, integration, tensor products, permutations, and contractions are computed in the model with the Koszul signs.*\n\n3.  *Let $U$ be a finite-dimensional ordinary rational color space. A pure colored tensor in $$\\label{eq:colored-chow-space}\n     \\left(\n       \\eta^{\\otimes k}A^*(C^k)\n       \\otimes U^{\\otimes k}\\otimes\\mathrm{sgn}_k\n     \\right)_{\\mathfrak S_k}$$ is zero if and only if all its evaluations using the same ordinary linear map $z\\colon U\\to W_b$ in every slot are zero in $\\mathfrak B$. Finitely many rational maps $z$ suffice for each fixed $U,k$. The assertion holds in the base-support quotient as well.*\n\n*In particular, a product of projected cubic entries is tested by products of $\\varphi(z(i),z(j),z(k))$, with the fixed Koszul sign coming from the ordering of the factors. A vanishing established by these tests is a vanishing in Chow, with the stated base-support bound.",
        "source_id": "PM-053.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-tensor-test",

The classes $1_C,e'_C$ correspond to $1,e$, and $\\eta$ projects onto $W_b$. Integration gives the hyperbolic pairing $\\langle1,e\\rangle=1$, $\\langle1,1\\rangle=\\langle e,e\\rangle=0$ on the two even summands and the symplectic pairing on $W_b$. All actual Chow correspondences between curve powers, not only pairing diagrams, are included faithfully in this model.*\n\n2.  *The projected small diagonal is an alternating three-form $$\\varphi\\colon W_b^3\\longrightarrow\\mathfrak B_{\\mathrm{odd}}.$$ Cup product, integration, tensor products, permutations, and contractions are computed in the model with the Koszul signs.*\n\n3.  *Let $U$ be a finite-dimensional ordinary rational color space. A pure colored tensor in $$\\label{eq:colored-chow-space}\n     \\left(\n       \\eta^{\\otimes k}A^*(C^k)\n       \\otimes U^{\\otimes k}\\otimes\\mathrm{sgn}_k\n     \\right)_{\\mathfrak S_k}$$ is zero if and only if all its evaluations using the same ordinary linear map $z\\colon U\\to W_b$ in every slot are zero in $\\mathfrak B$. Finitely many rational maps $z$ suffice for each fixed $U,k$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-coefficient-algebra",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F$ be the functor in <a href=\"#eq:symplectic-chow-functor\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:symplectic-chow-functor\">[eq:symplectic-chow-functor]</a>, and put $H(X)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_{\\mathcal C}(1,F(X))$. There is a supercommutative $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sp}}_{2b}$-algebra $\\mathfrak B$ such that, naturally in $X$, $$\\label{eq:coefficient-invariants}\n H(X)\\simeq(\\mathfrak B\\otimes X)^{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sp}}_{2b}}.$$ Under these identifications, tensor products of target morphisms become multiplication of coefficients. Arbitrary morphisms $F(X)\\to F(Y)$ are represented faithfully by equivariant $\\mathfrak B$-linear maps $\\mathfrak B\\otimes X\\to\\mathfrak B\\otimes Y$, and their composition is the usual tensor contraction.",
        "source_id": "PM-053.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-coefficient-algebra",
          "exact_quote": "Let $F$ be the functor in <a href=\"#eq:symplectic-chow-functor\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:symplectic-chow-functor\">[eq:symplectic-chow-functor]</a>, and put $H(X)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_{\\mathcal C}(1,F(X))$. There is a supercommutative $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sp}}_{2b}$-algebra $\\mathfrak B$ such that, naturally in $X$, $$\\label{eq:coefficient-invariants}\n H(X)\\simeq(\\mathfrak B\\otimes X)^{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sp}}_{2b}}.$$ Under these identifications, tensor products of target morphisms become multiplication of coefficients. Arbitrary morphisms $F(X)\\to F(Y)$ are represented faithfully by equivariant $\\mathfrak B$-linear maps $\\mathfrak B\\otimes X\\to\\mathfrak B\\otimes Y$, and their composition is the usual tensor contraction.",
          "source_id": "PM-053.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $F$ be the functor in <a href=\"#eq:symplectic-chow-functor\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:symplectic-chow-functor\">[eq:symplectic-chow-functor]</a>, and put $H(X)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_{\\mathcal C}(1,F(X))$. There is a supercommutative $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sp}}_{2b}$-algebra $\\mathfrak B$ such that, naturally in $X$, $$\\label{eq:coefficient-invariants}\n H(X)\\simeq(\\mathfrak B\\otimes X)^{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sp}}_{2b}}.$$ Under these identifications, tensor products of target morphisms become multiplication of coefficients. Arbitrary morphisms $F(X)\\to F(Y)$ are represented faithfully by equivariant $\\mathfrak B$-linear maps $\\mathfrak B\\otimes X\\to\\mathfrak B\\otimes Y$, and their composition is the usual tensor contraction.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-point-centered-algebra",
        "exact_quote": "Work modulo positive-codimension base support, and suppose $e_C$ is the class of a section. Then $e'_C=e_C$ in the quotient.         "source_id": "PM-053.1"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-quot-arrow",
        "exact_quote": "The derived stack of arrows with target $V$ has an open derived substack whose classical truncation is the ordinary relative Quot scheme with the prescribed Hilbert polynomial, before fixing determinant. On this truncation its relative tangent complex is $$\\operatorname{Cone}\\bigl(\n \\mathbf R\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(I',I')\\longrightarrow\n \\mathbf R\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(I',V)\\bigr)\n \\simeq \\mathbf R\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(I',Q).$$ This is a perfect complex of Tor-amplitude $[0,1]$, compatible with base change. The differential of forgetting the arrow and keeping its source is the boundary map $$\\mathbf R\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(I',Q)\\longrightarrow\\mathbf R\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(I',I')[1].$$",
        "source_id": "PM-053.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

The differential of forgetting the arrow and keeping its source is the boundary map $$\\mathbf R\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(I',Q)\\longrightarrow\\mathbf R\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(I',I')[1].$$",
          "source_id": "PM-053.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-marked-universality",
        "exact_quote": "Fix finitely many marked output surface factors. Push forward, over relative point Hilbert schemes and any additional surface factors, a finite polynomial in Chern characters of universal point ideals, their derived duals, line classes, relative tangent Chern classes, and equality diagonals. The resulting Chow class is a universal finite sum of decorated equality diagonals among the outputs, multiplied by relative characteristic pushes from closed surface components. One fixed polynomial procedure chooses the expression, independently of the surface family. No marked output is integrated in the procedure.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-weighted-pullback",
        "exact_quote": "Let $g\\geq2$, let the heavy set be empty, and pull the three-light operations to any larger number of light labels by forgetting. Under weight reduction from ordinary stable pointed curves, $[i=j]$ pulls back to $\\bar D_{ij}$ and $\\beta_{g,\\varnothing}$ pulls back to the normalized two-edge bridge sum used to define $\\beta_J^{\\mathrm{st}}$. Consequently the coefficient of the prescribed color word in the signed product of all $F^0_{g,\\varnothing}$ pulls back to $q(Y)$, up to a nonzero rational scalar.",
        "source_id": "PM-053.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-controlled-lifts",
        "exact_quote": "For every stable heavy type $(h,B)$ one can choose a symmetric class $G_{h,B}\\in A^2(X_{h,B}(3))$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *Over the smooth heavy base, $G_{h,B}$ is exactly the pullback of $\\Delta_C^{\\mathrm{pure}}$ by the coarse-position map.*\n\n2.  *Pull a raw cubic $F^0$, or one of these chosen pure lifts $G$, from any ancestor chart to a compact descendant cut chart. Its operation is a sum of the chosen pure lifts at the relevant descendant vertices, with all three labels assigned to that vertex, together with pair-times-singleton operations and a residual cubic operation.*\n\n3.  *The singleton operations in <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span>, and all their further pullbacks through cuts and light forgetting, are controlled as follows. Over fixed smooth core data their restrictions lie componentwise in the span of the unit, the canonical class, and the flag points. On any smooth test family the same statement holds modulo classes supported over positive base codimension.*\n\n4.  *On the current smooth-core open the residual operation in <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span> is a finite sum of classes containing a pulled base divisor factor. The base divisor may come from the current heavy base or from a compact ancestor base. Consequently its pullback to every smooth test family is zero operationally after removing a positive-codimension subset of that test base.*\n\n*All expansions preserve the color multiset. They may always use one and the same chosen $G$ and centering at a given current vertex, regardless of which ancestor supplied the operation.",
        "source_id": "PM-053.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-controlled-lifts",

Its operation is a sum of the chosen pure lifts at the relevant descendant vertices, with all three labels assigned to that vertex, together with pair-times-singleton operations and a residual cubic operation.*\n\n3.  *The singleton operations in <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span>, and all their further pullbacks through cuts and light forgetting, are controlled as follows. Over fixed smooth core data their restrictions lie componentwise in the span of the unit, the canonical class, and the flag points.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-support-advancement",
        "exact_quote": "Let a cycle multiplier on $X^\\Gamma(n)$ have base image contained in an irreducible closed subspace $Z\\subset\\mathcal B_\\Gamma$. After passing to a refinement if necessary, suppose that its generic base image lies in $\\mathcal S_\\Gamma$. Let $L$ be a factor involving a specified subset of the active entries.*\n\n*Suppose that the pull of $L$ vanishes operationally over a dense open of a smooth test family dominating $Z\\cap\\mathcal S_\\Gamma$. Then its action can be written as a finite sum of cycles with strictly smaller base-image dimension. Only the entries in $L$ are consumed.*\n\n*More precisely, take a smooth test base $S$, proper and generically finite over $Z\\cap\\mathcal S_\\Gamma$. If the vanishing holds off base codimension at least $a$, the terms remaining over $\\mathcal S_\\Gamma$ have base-image dimension at most $\\dim Z-a$. The other terms lie over $Z\\setminus\\mathcal S_\\Gamma$ and require a strict graph refinement. If one first replaces $S$ by the total space of one of its curve families to obtain a section, the corresponding bound is $\\dim Z-a+1$.",
        "source_id": "PM-053.1"
      },

        {

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-repeated-color",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that a vertex of genus $h$ has a flag and that $k>\\binom{h+1}{2}$ pure entries contain one common color $i$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
```
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Pixton's original span** ([source](#stmt-def-pixton-span)): Let $\sigma=(\sigma_1,\ldots,\sigma_\ell)$ be a partition with no part congruent to $2$ modulo $3$, possibly empty, and let $a_1,\ldots,a_n\geq0$ also avoid that residue class. For $0\leq r\leq3g-3+n$, require $$\label{eq:pixton-admissibility} 3r\geq g+1+\|\sigma\|+\sum_i a_i,\qquad 3r\equiv g+1+\|\sigma\|+\sum_i a_i\pmod2.$$ Set $$\begin{aligned} P_\Gamma={}& \left[ 2^{-h^1(\Gamma)} \widehat\kappa_\Gamma \left( \exp\{1-\widehat C_0\} \prod_{j=1}^{\ell}\{\widehat C_{\sigma_j}\} \right)\right.\notag\\ &\left.\hspace{12mm}\cdot \prod_{i=1}^n\widehat C_{a_i}(\psi_iT,\zeta_{v(i)}) \prod_{e\in E(\Gamma)}\Delta_e \right]_{T^{r-\|E(\Gamma)\|}\prod_v\zeta_v^{g_v+1}}, \label{eq:pixton-graph} \end{aligned}$$ where $h^1(\Gamma)=\|E\|-\|V\|+1$, $v(i)$ carries the $i$-th leg, and parity exponents are read modulo $2$. In braces, the arguments of $\widehat C_i$ are $T,\zeta$. A negative requested $T$-degree means zero. The relation is $$\mathcal R(g,n,r;\sigma,a)= \sum_\Gamma\frac{[\Gamma,P_\Gamma]}{\|\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\Gamma\|}.$$ The space $\mathcal P_{g,n}\subseteq S_{g,n}$ is the following linear span in each degree. Choose an outer stable graph of type $(g,n)$; put one such relation at one vertex, and arbitrary decorated strata elements at all other vertices; then glue, using the unnormalized gluing operation. Take the span over every choice, including the graph with one vertex and no edges.
- **Explicit counterexample class Y** ([source](#stmt-def-candidate)): On three labeled slots $J=(a,b,c)$, take every one-edge *unmarked* stable graph of genus $g$, subdivide its edge by a genus-zero vertex, and put all three labels at that new vertex. Sum the resulting undecorated graphs, dividing each by its automorphism order. Pull this sum back by forgetting all labels outside $J$; call the resulting class $\beta_J^{\mathrm{st}}$. Define $$\label{eq:candidate} F_J^{\mathrm{st}}= \bar D_{ab}\bar D_{ac}-\frac12\beta_J^{\mathrm{st}}, \qquad Y=\sum_{\substack{\tau\in\mathfrak S_n\\\tau\mathbf i=\mathbf i}} \operatorname{sgn}(\tau)\, \tau\left(\prod_J F_J^{\mathrm{st}}\right).$$ Here $\tau$ relabels slots, and products and forgetting pullbacks are the formal graph operations fixed above.
- **Strata algebra** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Let $S_{g,n}$ be the strata algebra of formal decorated stable graphs, with decoration degree truncated at the dimension of each vertex factor.
- **Color word** ([source](#pm-h-13)): Form a word $\mathbf i$ in the colors $1,\ldots,g$ by concatenating all increasing triples of colors in lexicographic order.
- **Tail divisor** ([source](#pm-h-13)): For a set of labels $T$ of size at least two, let $D_T$ be the one-edge class cutting off a genus-zero tail with precisely those labels.
- **Rational-tails graph** ([source](#pm-h-16)): Call a graph a *rational-tails graph* if it is a tree with one genus-$g$ vertex, called its root, and all other vertices rational.
- **Pure cubic entry** ([source](#pm-h-47)): A *pure cubic entry* is the class of the projected small diagonal in $\mathcal A_3(E)$ with a specified triple of color inputs; products use disjoint curve slots.
- **Entry** ([source](#sec:support)): An *entry* is one factor with three specified color inputs.
- **Noise entries** ([source](#pm-h-55)): Call the pair-times-singleton terms *noise entries*.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **s-detector-algebra** [claim](#stmt-lem-detector-algebra) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-detector-algebra): Direct verification of associativity and handle element from the pairing and alternating cubic -> Frobenius algebra
- **s-detector-cutoff** [claim](#stmt-lem-detector-cutoff) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-detector-cutoff): Coassociativity and dimension counting on rational branches -> well defined tests on the truncated algebra
- **s-detector-operations** [claim](#stmt-lem-detector-operations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-detector-operations): Boundary restriction formulas and projection formula -> action of bar classes and tail divisors
- **s-detector-forgetting** [claim](#stmt-lem-detector-forgetting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-detector-forgetting): Telescoping identities and local vertex calculation -> forgetting compatibility
- **s-detector-unit** [claim](#stmt-lem-detector-unit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-detector-unit): Kappa pullback and bubble correction computation -> zero-leg identity
- **s-detector-annihilation** [claim](#stmt-lem-detector-annihilation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-detector-annihilation): Lexicographic induction and an alternating-form count -> tests vanish on the span
- **s-detector-proposition** [claim](#stmt-prop-detector) / [proof](#pm-h-25): Evaluate the test on Y against Lagrangian vectors -> Y outside the span
- **s-relative-pfaffian** [claim](#stmt-lem-relative-pfaffian) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-relative-pfaffian): Fourier transform on the relative Jacobian and polynomial interpolation -> matching-sum vanishing
- **s-coefficient-algebra** [claim](#stmt-lem-coefficient-algebra) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-coefficient-algebra): Semisimple tensor reconstruction -> faithful coefficient algebra
- **s-color-polarization** [claim](#stmt-lem-color-polarization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-color-polarization): Polynomial interpolation on rational points -> same-map test
- **s-tensor-test** [claim](#stmt-prop-tensor-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-color-polarization-2): Assemble hyperbolic splitting, pfaffian relation, coefficient algebra and polarization -> faithful tensor test
- **s-quot-arrow** [claim](#stmt-lem-quot-arrow) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-quot-arrow): Derived arrow stack openness and tangent computation -> Quot tangent complex
- **s-det-surjective** [claim](#stmt-lem-det-surjective) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-det-surjective): Duality and ampleness -> surjective determinant obstruction map
- **s-point-excess** [claim](#stmt-lem-point-excess) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-point-excess): Comparison of tangent theories and excess intersection -> low virtual class with excess Euler factor
- **s-marked-universality** [claim](#stmt-lem-marked-universality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-marked-universality): Nested Hilbert scheme recursion keeping marked outputs -> universal equality-diagonal expression
- **s-localization** [claim](#stmt-prop-localization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-localization): Marked virtual localization and codimension counting -> scalar times nilpotent power vanishes
- **s-moment-additivity** [claim](#stmt-lem-moment-additivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-moment-additivity): Multiplicativity over disjoint unions and interpolation -> exponential form
- **s-scalar** [claim](#stmt-prop-scalar) / [proof](#pm-h-46): Residue computation with sign-definite coefficients -> nonzero scalar
- **s-surface-bound** [claim](#stmt-thm-surface) / [proof](#pm-h-46): Divide the localization relation by the nonzero scalar -> nilpotence of the isotropic contraction
- **s-exterior-ideal** [claim](#stmt-lem-exterior-ideal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-exterior-ideal): Symplectic Lefschetz decomposition -> ideal contains high degrees
- **s-large-vertex-test** [claim](#stmt-lem-large-vertex-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-large-vertex-test): Isotropic subspace, compound matrix and exterior ideal bound -> vanishing of long arbitrary-color products
- **s-weighted-pullback** [claim](#stmt-lem-weighted-pullback) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-weighted-pullback): Multiplicity count under weight reduction -> weighted cubic pulls back to Y
- **s-three-position-blowup** [claim](#stmt-lem-three-position-blowup) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-three-position-blowup): Principal ideal and ratio recovery arguments -> iterated blowup description
- **s-triple-cancellation** [claim](#stmt-lem-triple-exceptional-cancellation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-triple-exceptional-cancellation): Integrate over the chain surface fiber -> vanishing triple coefficient
- **s-controlled-lifts** [claim](#stmt-prop-controlled-lifts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-controlled-lifts): Subtract structured exceptional corrections and extend by Chow localization -> pure lifts with controlled expansions
- **s-support-advancement** [claim](#stmt-lem-support-advancement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-support-advancement): Proper generically finite lifts and Chow localization -> lower base-image dimension
- **s-entry-tests** [claim](#stmt-lem-elementary-entry-tests) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-elementary-entry-tests): Dimension count of singleton and odd coefficient spans -> noise and small-genus advancements
- **s-repeated-color** [claim](#stmt-lem-repeated-color) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-repeated-color): Schur-Weyl row cutoff and symplectic Lefschetz injectivity -> repeated-color advancement
- **s-point-centering** [claim](#stmt-lem-point-centered-algebra) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-point-centered-algebra): Pullback along the section is multiplicative -> centered pairing
- **s-chow-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-thm-chow-vanishing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-chow-vanishing): Finite support induction using all advancement tests, then pullback -> Y realizes to zero in Chow
- **s-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-chow-vanishing-2): Combine Chow vanishing with formal nonmembership -> Y in kernel but outside span
- **s-known-relations** [claim](#stmt-prop-known-relations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-known-relations): Cite stability and seed validity, then formal generation -> span in kernel
- **s-cohomological-corollary** [claim](#stmt-cor-cohomological-incompleteness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-cohomological-incompleteness): Apply cycle class compatibility -> cohomological incompleteness

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-residue-positive**: The coefficient of v^{27\chi-5} in (1+v^2)^{25\chi-1}(1-v)^{-24\chi}, which is the residue at zero used to show the bare-triple scalar is nonzero, is a strictly positive rational number, because every factor has nonnegative coefficients and the first has constant term one.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-geometric-claim**: Treating the passing series-coefficient check as evidence that the class Y vanishes in Chow or lies outside Pixton's span. -> The check covers only the positivity of one power-series coefficient. It says nothing about the Quot-scheme localization, the faithful Chow tensor model, the formal detector or the support induction at the stated genus and marking count.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The only finite constructive step of the paper is the explicit coefficient extraction in the residue at zero that proves the bare-triple scalar nonzero; this is an exact series coefficient computed by a finite procedure. The geometric results themselves are not finite constructions and are not tested.

```python
# INPUTS: one line on stdin with three non-negative integers A B n.
# PRECONDITIONS: A, B, n are integers >= 0.
# OUTPUTS: one integer, the coefficient of v^n in (1+v^2)^A * (1-v)^(-B).
# TIME: O((A+B)*n) big-integer additions.
# SPACE: O(n) big integers.
# ARITHMETIC: exact Python integers, no floating point.
# FAILURE: malformed input raises ValueError and exits with nonzero status.
import sys

def main():
    A, B, n = map(int, sys.stdin.read().split())
    s = [0] * (n + 1)
    s[0] = 1
    for _ in range(A):
        t = s[:]
        for i in range(2, n + 1):
            t[i] += s[i - 2]
        s = t
    for _ in range(B):
        for i in range(1, n + 1):
            s[i] += s[i - 1]
    print(s[n])

main()
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves that a formal class lies in the Chow kernel but outside the original Pixton span. It specifies no program, interface or data format. The only parameters involved are of extreme theoretical scale, so nothing here can be run as a library or regression fixture. Computer-algebra packages for tautological relations may only take the caveat that the original span is not the whole kernel. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source contains no dynamical system, stochastic process, discrete-time state update or physical model. All objects are formal graph sums and Chow classes of moduli stacks. There is no simulation semantics to import. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No tool, checker or file format is defined. The detector maps are described as tests on formal strata and are not asserted to factor through the Chow ring, so they cannot serve as a general-purpose relation verifier. A checker could at most mirror the finite graph identities of the detector at small arity, and the paper does not construct that. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The detector is defined by explicit rules on rational-tail graphs, but the paper gives no algorithm with stated cost, and the existence statement about vanishing is proved by induction on arity, degree and partition length, not by a finite computation. The support induction is a proof device bounded only by an advancement count, not a procedure with a running-time claim. There is no finite constructive output to execute and test. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Decorated stable graphs and the strata algebra are formal mathematical objects. The paper imposes no representation, indexing or update discipline. The huge parameters would also preclude any explicit encoding of the class. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the source concerns arithmetic kernels, memory hierarchies or parallel execution. The relevant computations are symbolic and take place in exterior and symplectic algebras, with no data-parallel structure identified. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The text has no adversary, hardness assumption, key, or protocol. The only exact statement of security flavour would be absent. Chow and cohomological incompleteness has no bearing on cryptographic constructions. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no dataset, loss, model class or learning guarantee. The color-polarization lemma is an injectivity statement for polynomial tensors, not a statement about learnability or generalisation. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No objective, feasibility region or solver appears. The color budget in the support induction is a counting argument to show enough unused color blocks exist, not an allocation problem to be optimised. Its constants are explicitly not proposed as minimal parameters. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source has no nodes, messages, failure model or consensus notion. Graph vertices and edges are strata of nodal curves, not processes or channels, so graph identities here say nothing about scheduling or dependency graphs. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is a purely mathematical incompleteness theorem in algebraic geometry. Its direct value lies in settling the Chow and rational-cohomological forms of a completeness conjecture for the original relation span, and in the formal-versus-Chow dichotomy of cubic coefficients. It supplies no engineering artefact. It also does not decide completeness for generalised relation systems or low genus. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **hyp-fixed-huge-parameters** (hypothesis): The incompleteness is established only for the single stable pair with the fixed huge genus and marking count of the main theorem. It is not shown for any other pair. Blocked use: ['Concluding that the Pixton span fails to equal the kernel for a given smaller genus or marking count', 'Using the class Y as an executable test case']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We disprove the Chow and rational-cohomological forms of Pixton's completeness conjecture for his original relation system. We construct a formal tautological class outside the original Pixton relation span whose image is zero in the Chow ring and in rational cohomology. The example has genus $`10^{60}`$ and $`3\binom{10^{60}}3`$ markings.

| [stmt-lem-triple-exceptional-cancellation](#stmt-lem-triple-exceptional-cancellation) | lemma | `lem:triple-exceptional-cancellation` | 30 | For the pullback of a raw cubic class $F^0$ from an ancestor chart, the coefficient $a_p$ in [eq:blowup-components] is zero at every triple flag locus.* |
| [stmt-prop-controlled-lifts](#stmt-prop-controlled-lifts) | proposition | `prop:controlled-lifts` | 31 | For every stable heavy type $(h,B)$ one can choose a symmetric class $G_{h,B}\in A^2(X_{h,B}(3))$ with the following properties.* |
| [stmt-rem-family-not-fiber](#stmt-rem-family-not-fiber) | remark | `rem:family-not-fiber` | - | Remark 32*. The conclusion in Proposition 31(iv) is stronger than vanishing on individual geometric fibers. |

| [stmt-lem-elementary-entry-tests](#stmt-lem-elementary-entry-tests) | lemma | `lem:elementary-entry-tests` | 34 | Set $$R_\Gamma=2&#124;V(\Gamma)&#124;+2&#124;E(\Gamma)&#124;.$$ If $R_\Gamma+1$ noise entries have their singleton at the same color, their product advances support while consuming those entries. |
| [stmt-lem-repeated-color](#stmt-lem-repeated-color) | lemma | `lem:repeated-color` | 35 | Suppose that a vertex of genus $h$ has a flag and that $k>\binom{h+1}{2}$ pure entries contain one common color $i$. |

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

The tautological ring of the moduli space of stable curves is generated by cotangent and kappa classes, together with the classes obtained by forgetting markings and gluing nodes. Describing all relations among these natural classes is a central problem in the intersection theory of moduli spaces. Pixton proposed a uniform system of stable-graph relations and conjectured that it is complete. We construct a relation outside that system: an alternating product of corrected small diagonals that vanishes in rational Chow, and therefore in rational cohomology.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The relation problem

Mumford initiated the systematic study of natural classes in the Chow ring of the moduli space of curves <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Mumford1983](#ref-Mumford1983), Sections 4–5]. Faber’s calculations and geometric constructions on powers of the universal curve led to a proposed description of the tautological ring of the smooth unpointed space $\mathcal M_g$, including its Gorenstein property <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Faber1999](#ref-Faber1999), Conjectures 1–2]. Faber and Zagier later gave explicit hypergeometric formulas for relations among the kappa classes. Pandharipande–Pixton proved these relations in Chow using stable quotients and formulated their completeness on $\mathcal M_g$ <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[PandharipandePixton2021](#ref-PandharipandePixton2021), Theorem 1 and Conjecture 2].

On the compactification, a presentation must also account for boundary strata and their intersections. Arbarello–Cornalba and Graber–Pandharipande developed the forgetful and boundary calculus <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[ArbarelloCornalba1996](#ref-ArbarelloCornalba1996), Section 1], <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[GraberPandharipande2003](#ref-GraberPandharipande2003), Appendix A]; in genus zero, Keel gave a presentation of the entire Chow ring by boundary divisors <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Keel1992](#ref-Keel1992), Section 4, Theorem 1]. Pixton’s 2012 graph formula extends the Faber–Zagier system to $\overline{\mathcal M}_{g,n}$ and proposes a complete set of relations <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Pixton](#ref-Pixton), Sections 1–3, Conjecture 2]. Pandharipande–Pixton–Zvonkine proved the cohomological validity of this system using the $3$-spin CohFT <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[PPZ](#ref-PPZ)]; Janda proved its Chow validity using equivariant Gromov–Witten theory of $\mathbb P^1$ <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Janda](#ref-Janda)]. These results establish relations, while completeness asks whether any further relations exist.

We fix the original relation space precisely. Let $S_{g,n}$ be the strata algebra of formal decorated stable graphs, with decoration degree truncated at the dimension of each vertex factor. Its realization is $$q_{g,n}\colon S_{g,n}\longrightarrow A^*(\overline{\mathcal M}_{g,n};\mathbb Q),
\qquad [\Gamma,\gamma]\longmapsto\xi_{\Gamma*}\gamma.$$ The gluing pushforward generators are unnormalized, as in <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[PPZ](#ref-PPZ), Section 0.3]. Let $\mathcal P_{g,n}$ be the span obtained by placing one of Pixton’s graph-formula relations at a vertex of an outer stable graph, placing arbitrary strata classes at the other vertices, and gluing. Pixton’s Proposition 1 shows that this span is already an ideal, stable under the gluing and forgetting pushforwards and pullbacks <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Pixton](#ref-Pixton)]. Thus it includes these formal consequences from the outset. The smaller $0/1$-marking seed system recovers the same span under the operations described in <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[PPZ](#ref-PPZ), Section 3.5]. Section <a href="#sec:strata" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:strata">2</a> gives the full recipe and all conventions.

Writing $R^*(\overline{\mathcal M}_{g,n})=\mathop{\mathrm{im}}q_{g,n}$, the Chow completeness question is whether $$\ker q_{g,n}=\mathcal P_{g,n}$$ for every stable pair. The known validity theorem gives the inclusion $\mathcal P_{g,n}\subseteq\ker q_{g,n}$. Our example makes it strict.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The counterexample

The construction uses $g$ colors and one three-label factor for each increasing triple of colors. Each factor is a product of two rational-tail divisor sums, corrected by a class supported on two-node rational bridges. We multiply these factors and alternate over relabelings that preserve the color of each slot. Definition <a href="#def:candidate" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:candidate">5</a> specifies the resulting formal class $Y$.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Set $$g=10^{60},\qquad D_*=\binom g3,\qquad n=3D_*.$$ The explicitly defined class $Y\in S_{g,n}^{2D_*}$ of Definition <a href="#def:candidate" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:candidate">5</a> satisfies $$q_{g,n}(Y)=0,\qquad Y\notin\mathcal P_{g,n}.$$ Consequently Pixton’s original relation span does not exhaust the tautological Chow relations: the equality $\ker q_{g,n}=\mathcal P_{g,n}$ fails for this stable pair.*

</div>

The same class gives the rational-cohomological consequence. To specify that formulation, write $q^{\mathrm{CH}}_{g,n}=q_{g,n}$ and define, in formal degree $d$, $$q^H_{g,n}=\operatorname{cl}\circ q^{\mathrm{CH}}_{g,n}
\colon S^d_{g,n}\longrightarrow
H^{2d}\!\left(\overline{\mathcal M}_{g,n}(\mathbb C);\mathbb Q\right),$$ where $\operatorname{cl}$ is the rational cycle-class map for the complex Deligne–Mumford stack. Its image is the cohomological tautological ring $RH^*=\operatorname{cl}(R^*)$. Completeness in both $R^*$ and $RH^*$ is explicitly recorded as Pixton’s conjecture in <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Canning2025](#ref-Canning2025), Section 1, Conjecture 1].

<a id="stmt-cor-cohomological-incompleteness"></a>
<div id="cor:cohomological-incompleteness" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2** (Rational-cohomological incompleteness). *For the stable pair and class $Y$ of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, $$q^H_{g,n}(Y)=0
\quad\text{in }H^{4D_*}\!\left(\overline{\mathcal M}_{g,n}(\mathbb C);\mathbb Q\right),
\qquad
Y\notin\mathcal P_{g,n}.$$ In particular, $\mathcal P_{g,n}\subsetneq\ker q^H_{g,n}$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-cohomological-incompleteness"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-cohomological-incompleteness</summary>

*Proof.* Cycle class commutes with Chern classes, products, and proper pushforward. In particular, $\operatorname{cl}(\xi_{\Gamma*}\gamma)
=\xi_{\Gamma*}\operatorname{cl}(\gamma)$, and the same compatibility applies to the forgetting-pushforward definition of kappa classes. Thus $q^H_{g,n}$ is the cohomological graph realization of <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[PPZ](#ref-PPZ), Section 0.3], with the same vertexwise truncation and unnormalized generators. Applying cycle class to Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> gives $q^H_{g,n}(Y)=0$; nonmembership remains an assertion in the same formal strata algebra. The strict inclusion follows from $$\mathcal P_{g,n}\subseteq\ker q^{\mathrm{CH}}_{g,n}
\subseteq\ker q^H_{g,n}.$$ Passing from Chow vanishing to cohomological vanishing uses the second inclusion; it requires no injectivity of cycle class. ◻

</details>

The theorem and corollary give negative answers to the Chow and rational-cohomological completeness questions for the full original system. The large parameters leave ample room in the support and color estimates; they are not proposed as minimal parameters.

Completeness is distinct from the Gorenstein property. The latter asks for nondegenerate multiplication pairings in complementary degrees, whereas completeness asks for all relations among the generators. Pixton proved that $R^*(\mathcal M_{25})$ is not Gorenstein while establishing Faber–Zagier completeness there <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Pixton2026](#ref-Pixton2026), Theorem 1.1 and Section 2.2]. Canning–Larson–Schmitt proved completeness together with non-Gorenstein behavior on the compact-type spaces $\mathcal M^{\mathrm{ct}}_6$, $\mathcal M^{\mathrm{ct}}_{5,2}$, and $\mathcal M^{\mathrm{ct}}_7$ <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[CanningLarsonSchmitt2026](#ref-CanningLarsonSchmitt2026), Theorems 1 and 7]. Our relation lies on the full stable compactification.

There is also a distinction between completeness and comparison of methods for producing relations. Janda’s comparison theorem identifies the systems arising from cancellation of reconstruction singularities for convergent semisimple CohFTs with nonempty discriminant <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[JandaDiscriminant](#ref-JandaDiscriminant), Definition 3.3.1 and Theorem 3.3.6]. It concerns the relations generated by those reconstructions; it does not identify the entire Chow relation kernel. The comparison throughout this paper is with the explicitly fixed span $\mathcal P_{g,n}$.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Proof and methods

The proof compares two algebras of cubic coefficients. A formal super Frobenius algebra can carry independent odd cubic coefficients while satisfying the identities needed to annihilate the original Pixton span. The actual Chow correspondences of a curve satisfy an additional nilpotence identity obtained from a surface Quot scheme. The first algebra proves nonmembership; the second proves vanishing. Figure <a href="#fig:proof-map" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:proof-map">1</a> separates these two assertions about $Y$.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
###### The formal test.

Section <a href="#sec:detector" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:detector">3</a> constructs a linear map on decorated graphs by discarding graphs outside the rational-tails locus and evaluating rational branches by Frobenius multiplication and coproduct. This uses the graph calculus familiar from two-dimensional topological field theory <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Abrams1996](#ref-Abrams1996), Section 2 and Theorem 3]. The odd symplectic curve pairing and the vanishing of contractions between isotropically supported corrections have precedents in Pandharipande–Zvonkine’s CohFT constructions <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[PZ2019](#ref-PZ2019), Sections 1.1–1.5 and Remark 11]. Here the coefficient ring is the exterior algebra on independent $\theta_{ijk}$, one for every color triple; we verify the graph identities directly. Every element of $\mathcal P_{g,n}$ has zero test, while the test of $Y$ has a nonzero coefficient proportional to $\prod_{i<j<k}\theta_{ijk}$.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
###### The smooth-family Chow identity.

Section <a href="#sec:tensors" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:tensors">4</a> splits the correspondence object of a smooth curve into two even summands and an odd symplectic summand. The coefficient-algebra construction is a concrete form of O’Sullivan’s semisimple tensor reconstruction <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[OSullivan2011](#ref-OSullivan2011), Lemma 2.3.1]. It retains all actual relative Chow correspondences, so its faithfulness is an assertion about Chow, not about cohomological realization. The small diagonal projected to three odd summands gives an alternating cubic $\varphi$ with odd coefficients. Over a pointed curve this is the modified diagonal of Gross–Schoen <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[GrossSchoen1995](#ref-GrossSchoen1995), Equation (0.1) and Sections 2–3]; the family construction also retains the necessary base corrections.

Sections <a href="#sec:quot" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:quot">5</a>–<a href="#sec:coefficient" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:coefficient">6</a> prove the additional nilpotence. The surface is the square of the curve. A relative Quot scheme with fixed determinant produces a relation for products of the cubic on isotropic vector sets. Its obstruction theory uses derived perfect-complex moduli and determinant maps <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[TV](#ref-TV), [STV](#ref-STV)], virtual pullbacks <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[BF](#ref-BF), [Manolache](#ref-Manolache)], and virtual localization <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[GP](#ref-GP)]. Surface Quot localization and relative Chow identities appear in Marian–Oprea–Pandharipande <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[MOPSegre](#ref-MOPSegre)]; the point obstruction factors have the antecedent in <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[OPQuot](#ref-OPQuot), Lemma 34 and Section 5.2]. Here the calculation keeps the determinant constraint, arbitrary irregularity, and every marked surface output.

The nested-Hilbert recursion of Ellingsrud–Göttsche–Lehn <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[EGL](#ref-EGL)] reduces that marked calculation to decorated equality diagonals. Kong develops relative forms of this recursion and, for curve families, formulas retaining one and two marked outputs <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[Kong2026](#ref-Kong2026), Section 2.1 and Lemma 3.4]. We prove the version retaining an arbitrary finite set of surface outputs. Only bare triple blocks survive the relevant codimension bound. Section <a href="#sec:coefficient" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:coefficient">6</a> computes their common scalar and proves it is nonzero, using symmetric-power and residue methods related to <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[MOPCombinatorics](#ref-MOPCombinatorics), Equation (9), Theorem 2, and Lemma 3]. It then converts the isotropic identity into vanishing tests for sufficiently long products with arbitrary color inputs (Lemma <a href="#lem:large-vertex-test" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:large-vertex-test">27</a>). Finite faithful polarization returns a Chow identity modulo classes supported over the prescribed positive codimension in the base.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
###### Passage to the compactification.

Section <a href="#sec:compactification" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:compactification">7</a> compares the corrected stable cubic with these projected smooth-curve cubics. The framework uses Hassett’s weighted stable curves <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[Hassett](#ref-Hassett)] and configuration-space blowups <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[FultonMacPherson](#ref-FultonMacPherson), [Routis](#ref-Routis)]; Petersen’s proof of Tavakol’s presentation conjecture treats the corresponding rational-tails comparison over a smooth core <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[TavakolRT](#ref-TavakolRT), [PetersenWonderful](#ref-PetersenWonderful)]. The nodal comparison here requires a rational-bridge correction with coefficient $-1/2$. A three-position calculation shows that it cancels the remaining triple-exceptional contribution. The errors are then either a two-slot operation times a one-slot operation from a bounded span, or operations supported over positive base codimension.

Section <a href="#sec:support" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:support">8</a> uses these errors to lower the dimension of the image of the supporting cycle in the base. Chow-localization support methods occur in Graber–Vakil’s filtration by rational components <a id="pm-cite-33"></a>[[GraberVakil](#ref-GraberVakil), Theorem 1.1 and Section 4.2]; the induction here acts on arbitrary cycle multipliers over the base parametrizing the curve components. When one component carries almost all the genus, the surface identity advances support. Otherwise a repeated-color identity supplies a smaller vanishing factor, using the curve algebra centered at a marked node branch, Schur–Weyl alternation, and symplectic contraction. The final color budget leaves enough entries for every advancement, until the support is empty. Pullback from the weighted space then proves $q_{g,n}(Y)=0$.

Thus Sections <a href="#sec:strata" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:strata">2</a>–<a href="#sec:detector" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:detector">3</a> construct and detect the formal candidate; Sections <a href="#sec:tensors" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:tensors">4</a>–<a href="#sec:coefficient" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:coefficient">6</a> prove the smooth-family identity; and Sections <a href="#sec:compactification" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:compactification">7</a>–<a href="#sec:support" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:support">8</a> extend its vanishing to the compactification. Table <a href="#tab:notation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tab:notation">1</a> collects the notation used across these parts.

<figure id="fig:proof-map">

<figcaption>The two assertions about the same class $Y$. The formal branch is independent of Chow vanishing. In the geometric branch, the nonzero scalar completes the isotropic surface bound. The exterior-ideal argument then gives arbitrary-color vanishing modulo base support before the passage to the compactification.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Conventions

All varieties, stacks, and families are over $\mathbb C$, and Chow groups have rational coefficients. On a smooth space we identify Chow classes with operational classes when pulling them to a test space. Products of stacks are understood in rational Chow theory . We use proper rational pushforward for relative Deligne–Mumford maps and its compatibilities from . Smooth Deligne–Mumford stacks have the operational identification in ; for smooth quotient stacks, see also . Every formal series calculation is truncated at a specified coefficient, so only finitely many terms occur.

Parity is distinct from Chow codimension. In a super tensor product, interchanging homogeneous factors of parities $p,q$ gives $(-1)^{pq}$. In particular, on an odd vector space the super-symmetric tensors are ordinary alternating tensors. All finite averaging and polarization arguments take place in characteristic zero.

<a id="sec:strata"></a>
### The strata algebra and the explicit class

This section fixes the relation space. The distinction between an unnormalized gluing generator and an automorphism-weighted graph sum will be used in both parts of the proof.

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### Decorated graphs

A stable graph $\Gamma$ of type $(g,n)$ is a connected graph with $n$ labeled legs and genera $g_v\geq0$ at its vertices, satisfying $$g=\sum_{v}g_v+|E(\Gamma)|-|V(\Gamma)|+1,
\qquad 2g_v-2+n_v>0.$$ Here $n_v$ counts all incident half-edges, including legs. Its gluing map is $$\xi_\Gamma\colon\prod_{v\in V(\Gamma)}
\overline{\mathcal M}_{g_v,n_v}\longrightarrow\overline{\mathcal M}_{g,n}.$$ At every half-edge let $\psi$ denote the cotangent class. At a vertex define $$\kappa_j=\pi_*(\psi_{n_v+1}^{j+1}),\qquad
\kappa_0=2g_v-2+n_v,$$ where $\pi$ forgets an additional marking.

The vector space $S_{g,n}$ is spanned by formal symbols $[\Gamma,\gamma]$, modulo graph isomorphism, with $\gamma$ a monomial in the half-edge $\psi$’s and vertex $\kappa_j$’s for $j\geq1$. At each vertex its decoration degree is at most $3g_v-3+n_v$. The total degree is $|E(\Gamma)|+\deg\gamma$, and $$q_{g,n}[\Gamma,\gamma]=\xi_{\Gamma*}(\gamma).$$ There is no automorphism denominator in this definition of $q_{g,n}$. The per-vertex degree bound is the explicit convention in <a id="pm-cite-34"></a>[[PPZ](#ref-PPZ), Section 0.3].

We use the usual decorated-graph intersection product. More explicitly, take common refinements with specified contractions to fixed representatives of the input graphs, requiring every refined edge to survive in at least one contraction. Divide by the source symmetries preserving those contractions. For each additional use of an overlapping edge insert its normal factor $-\psi-\psi'$; pull the leg and flag decorations to their components and split kappa classes additively. Specified contractions record the lifts to the gluing parameter spaces, so this convention gives the product of the *unnormalized* pushforward generators. The construction is the transverse and self-intersection rule for node cuts <a id="pm-cite-35"></a>[[GraberPandharipande2003](#ref-GraberPandharipande2003), Appendix A, Proposition 9 and Equation (11)].

Terms exceeding any vertex dimension are set to zero. For multiplication and restriction to a further graph splitting, an excessive decoration remains excessive: the combined dimensions on the pieces decrease, so at least one piece still exceeds its dimension. Forgetting pullback of an undecorated graph is obtained by adding the new leg successively at each vertex. These conventions lift the corresponding Chow operations and do not impose any unproved relation. The preceding dimension argument is not an assertion about forgetting, which raises a vertex dimension. Compatibility of forgetting with the formal detector is checked separately in Section <a href="#sec:detector" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:detector">3</a>.

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### The exact Pixton span

Define $$A(T)=\sum_{m\geq0}\frac{(6m)!}{(3m)!(2m)!}T^m,\qquad
B(T)=\sum_{m\geq0}
\frac{6m+1}{6m-1}\frac{(6m)!}{(3m)!(2m)!}T^m.$$ For a commuting parity variable $\zeta^2=1$, put $$\widehat C_{3j}(T,\zeta)=T^jA(\zeta T),\qquad
\widehat C_{3j+1}(T,\zeta)=\zeta T^jB(\zeta T).$$ Indices congruent to $2$ modulo $3$ are not used. For a series $F$ write $$\{F\}=\sum_{m\geq0,\ \epsilon\in\{0,1\}}
[F]_{T^m\zeta^\epsilon}K_{m,\epsilon}T^m.$$ Give every vertex of $\Gamma$ its own variable $\zeta_v$, with $\zeta_v^2=1$. Define the operator $$\widehat\kappa_\Gamma
\left(\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}K_{e_i,\epsilon_i}\right)
=
\sum_{\tau\in\mathfrak S_\ell}
\prod_{\substack{c\text{ a cycle}\\\text{of }\tau}}
\left(
\sum_{v\in V(\Gamma)}
\kappa^{(v)}_{\sum_{i\in c}e_i}
\zeta_v^{\sum_{i\in c}\epsilon_i}
\right).$$ The empty product maps to $1$, and the operator acts coefficientwise in $T$. The permutation-cycle expression is Faber’s formula, as recorded in <a id="pm-cite-36"></a>[[ArbarelloCornalba1996](#ref-ArbarelloCornalba1996), Equations (1.12)–(1.13)].

For an edge whose two half-edges lie at $v,w$, with cotangent symbols $x,y$, set $$\Delta_e=
\frac{
A(\zeta_vxT)\zeta_wB(\zeta_wyT)
+\zeta_vB(\zeta_vxT)A(\zeta_wyT)
+\zeta_v+\zeta_w
}{(x+y)T}.$$ This is a power series by $$A(T)B(-T)+A(-T)B(T)+2=0.$$ The same rule applies to a loop.

<a id="stmt-def-pixton-span"></a>
<div id="def:pixton-span" class="definition">

<a id="eq:pixton-admissibility"></a>
**Definition 3** (Pixton’s original span). Let $\sigma=(\sigma_1,\ldots,\sigma_\ell)$ be a partition with no part congruent to $2$ modulo $3$, possibly empty, and let $a_1,\ldots,a_n\geq0$ also avoid that residue class. For $0\leq r\leq3g-3+n$, require $$\label{eq:pixton-admissibility}
3r\geq g+1+|\sigma|+\sum_i a_i,\qquad
3r\equiv g+1+|\sigma|+\sum_i a_i\pmod2.$$ Set <a id="eq:pixton-graph"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
P_\Gamma={}&
\left[
2^{-h^1(\Gamma)}
\widehat\kappa_\Gamma
\left(
\exp\{1-\widehat C_0\}
\prod_{j=1}^{\ell}\{\widehat C_{\sigma_j}\}
\right)\right.\notag\\
&\left.\hspace{12mm}\cdot
\prod_{i=1}^n\widehat C_{a_i}(\psi_iT,\zeta_{v(i)})
\prod_{e\in E(\Gamma)}\Delta_e
\right]_{T^{r-|E(\Gamma)|}\prod_v\zeta_v^{g_v+1}},
\label{eq:pixton-graph}
\end{aligned}$$ where $h^1(\Gamma)=|E|-|V|+1$, $v(i)$ carries the $i$-th leg, and parity exponents are read modulo $2$. In braces, the arguments of $\widehat C_i$ are $T,\zeta$. A negative requested $T$-degree means zero. The relation is $$\mathcal R(g,n,r;\sigma,a)=
\sum_\Gamma\frac{[\Gamma,P_\Gamma]}{|\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\Gamma|}.$$

The space $\mathcal P_{g,n}\subseteq S_{g,n}$ is the following linear span in each degree. Choose an outer stable graph of type $(g,n)$; put one such relation at one vertex, and arbitrary decorated strata elements at all other vertices; then glue, using the unnormalized gluing operation. Take the span over every choice, including the graph with one vertex and no edges.

</div>

<a id="stmt-prop-known-relations"></a>
<div id="prop:known-relations" class="proposition">

**Proposition 4** (Known validity and stability). *The span in Definition <a href="#def:pixton-span" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:pixton-span">3</a> is an ideal and is stable under tautological gluing and forgetting pushforwards and pullbacks. For gluing pullback to a product, the target relation space is the sum of the spaces with a relation in one factor. Moreover $$\mathcal P_{g,n}\subseteq\ker q_{g,n}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-known-relations"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-known-relations</summary>

*Proof.* The ideal and operation statements are <a id="pm-cite-37"></a>[[Pixton](#ref-Pixton), Proposition 1] for precisely this original span. Chow validity of the $a_i\in\{0,1\}$ seed relations follows from <a id="pm-cite-38"></a>[[Janda](#ref-Janda), Theorem 1 and its reconstruction]. The construction in <a id="pm-cite-39"></a>[[PPZ](#ref-PPZ), Section 3.5] generates the higher marking indices by psi multiplication, the partition insertions by forgetting extra markings, and the outer-graph relations by gluing. These are formal identities, so they apply to the Chow-valid seeds. The parity condition in <a href="#eq:pixton-admissibility" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pixton-admissibility">[eq:pixton-admissibility]</a> converts the strict seed inequality to the displayed admissibility inequality. In Janda’s reconstructed normalization, a degree-$r$ seed is $(-1)^{\sum_i a_i}(-1/1728)^r$ times the empty-partition recipe above. This nonzero scalar does not change its vanishing or its span. ◻

</details>
